## CONFLICT KEYS MINTED IN THIS PART

Numbering is part-local (`S1P1-CF-nn`) and continues across volumes (§9.3); §U in the Q–U volume consolidates these with the dossier conflict IDs they restate. Line format per method §7.

S1P1-CF-01 **Which date is the company's, and where was it formed.** CLAIM A: incorporated in California on 1977-01-03 (S1P1-12, registrant filing; S1P1-13, 1981 print giving the year only). CLAIM B: a partnership agreement dated 1976-04-01 (S1P1-26), and the universe register's "1976-04-01, Los Altos" gloss traced to a Wikipedia raw page. / WHY THEY DIFFER: two legal events of different character nine months apart, plus an unsourced place, collapsed into one word. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: A and B are both primary in kind; the Los Altos cell is derivative and untraceable here; the catalogue's own bracket gives **Mountain View** (S1P1-27). / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: partnership 1976-04-01, corporation 1977-01-03, **two different events**; place of formation unresolved, premises UNKNOWN. / RESIDUAL UNCERTAINTY: the succession mechanics — whether the partnership became the corporation — are documented nowhere (C-01, C-07). / CONFIDENCE: High on both dates' existence, Medium on the day, Low on place. Restates U-AP-1, U-C-1.

S1P1-CF-02 **Which event is the Stage-1 boundary.** CLAIM A: the designer's demonstration to a club in late 1975 (folklore; unsupported — AP-26's null). CLAIM B: a first dealer order in July 1976 (S1P1-11; undocumented — S1P1-CF-03). CLAIM C: 1977-01-03, the first legal-entity date. / WHY THEY DIFFER: A and B are memoir-shaped and would place the boundary at a vivid scene; C is document-shaped and lands in a month the record leaves empty (S1P1-29). / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: C on two Tier-1 origins; A and B on none. / BEST-SUPPORTED: **C**, with the cost named — the strongest commercial artifacts then fall outside the window (S1P1-CF-10). / RESIDUAL: 1977-04 and 1977-06 are defensible alternative ends for a stage defined by market action rather than legal action; the probe's Stage 2 assigns them elsewhere and this part does not re-open that. / CONFIDENCE: High for the choice as made, Medium as to its optimality.

S1P1-CF-03 **The first product's price, the first order, and 1976 money.** CLAIM A (canon): 50 boards at $500 wholesale in July 1976 from the Byte Shop, retailed at $666.66, restated as fact by the 2026 auction essay (C-32). CLAIM B (this corpus): no in-window document names an order, a quantity, a unit price or a revenue figure; the only maker-side number is "a price under $700 at the retail level" (S1P1-32), and no 1976 print states any price (S1P1-20). / WHY THEY DIFFER: A is one merchant's later conversations, re-printed by a party selling the founding paper, and **contradicts itself** — RR Auction dates it Spring 1976, the canon says July; a Tier-4 post attributed to the same merchant says $525 wholesale, not $500. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: A is retrospective and self-conflicting; B is a documented null over ~13 MB. / BEST-SUPPORTED: **first order UNKNOWN; price UNKNOWN; FY1976–77 revenue UNKNOWN**, with the reason printed (no in-window document establishes it, and the paper 1980 prospectus is the only plausible carrier). / RESIDUAL UNCERTAINTY: an October 1976 Apple-1 advertisement image (D's DG-D2) or an OCR pass over Kilobaud 1976 (no text layer) could still settle the price. / CONFIDENCE: High on the absence, **not** on the true figures. Restates U-AP-2, U-A2-2, U-A2-4, U-C-4.

S1P1-CF-04 **Channel character: word of mouth, or a printed national circuit.** CLAIM A: sale "by word of mouth throughout California and later nationwide through retail computer stores" (S1P1-19, founder, contemporaneous). CLAIM B: BYTE's April 1977 directory of ~60 named outlets across 22 jurisdictions including Tokyo and Vancouver, plus Apple's own June 1977 national direct-mail order form taking bank cards (S1P1-31). / WHY THEY DIFFER: different subjects and different dates — A describes 1975–76 distribution of a board, B documents 1977 distribution of a system; and B's directory describes the **retail circuit**, not Apple's relationship to it, which no document evidences even for the advertisers who stocked the machine (S1P1-17). / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: A is one founder's compressed sequence; B is compiled third-party print plus one company's own instrument. / BEST-SUPPORTED: **the tension is the finding** — A's channel type is corroborated and its sequence is contradicted (L-10); B is not evidence against A because it post-dates A's subject. The transition between them has no document (S1P1-34). / RESIDUAL: whether any dealer held Apple product under agreement before April 1977 is unestablished either way. / CONFIDENCE: High that both statements were printed; Low on any resolution beyond the above.

S1P1-CF-05 **The sales-tax rate printed in the field.** CLAIM A: Apple's own June 1977 ladder implies 6.5% at four independent rows (`84.37 / 1298.00 = 0.0650`). CLAIM B: other advertisers in the same magazine print 6% in 1976 and 1980 (the Berkeley line inside S1P1-20's own source; the December 1980 advertisement). / WHY THEY DIFFER: different advertisers, different months, possibly different tax bases and periods. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: A is internally consistent across four rows of one maker's table; B is two other advertisers' copy. / BEST-SUPPORTED: Apple's own printed rate for Apple's own prices; the field figure is not a contradiction of it. / RESIDUAL: which rate was statutory in California in June 1977 is outside this corpus. / CONFIDENCE: High on both as printed. Restates U-A2-6.

S1P1-CF-06 **Markkula's entry, terms and status.** CLAIM A (canon, post-1981 books): a $91,000 bank guarantee, roughly $250,000 in, a 26–28% stake, arriving February 1977, as the 2026 catalogue puts it "the company's third co-founder". CLAIM B (corpus): one 1981 sentence giving name, age, holding and an editorial gloss; **zero** occurrences in any file dated 1975 through December 1980; his arrival undated in any record (S1P1-24). / WHY THEY DIFFER: A descends from book history this project has not read; B is the print record, which is silent on him by design — it printed share counts, not cap-table negotiations. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: none of A is documented here; B is a documented null. / BEST-SUPPORTED: **both sides recorded, neither resolved**: a founder-sized block existed by 1980-12 (Medium-High as printed); the terms and the arrival date are UNKNOWN. / RESIDUAL: no document in the corpus dates his arrival, and no later memoir may supply his 1976 status. / CONFIDENCE: High for the null, Low for the canon.

S1P1-CF-07 **Wayne's stake and exit interval.** CLAIM A: 10%, on a 45/45/10 allocation, withdrawn eleven days after formation (S1P1-26's two dates, 1976-04-01 → 1976-04-12, internally corroborating the interval). CLAIM B: 12% and "12 days", sold for $800, "no regrets" — the circulating canon, and separately "$800 plus a later $1,500" (AP-20). / WHY THEY DIFFER: 45/45/10 exhausts 100, so 12% cannot coexist with the equal split; "12 days" is a rounding of a dated instrument. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: A rests on a Tier-3 catalogue's reading of a Tier-1 object **this project has not read**; B rests on nothing citable. / BEST-SUPPORTED: **10%, eleven days** — with the standing caveat that the instrument is unread, so this is the best-supported figure and not a verified clause. / RESIDUAL: the consideration ($800 alone, or $800 then $1,500), the motivation, and whether Wayne's later litigation to sever his name from partnership debts is in any reachable court record. / CONFIDENCE: Medium-High on 10%; High that 12% is unsupported by any document; Low on the money. Restates U-C-4's percentage leg, U-AP-4.

S1P1-CF-08 **Officer titles: absent from the record, absent from the firm?** CLAIM A: no founder held a documented title in 1976–77 (S1P1-22, a full census). CLAIM B: the founding instrument allocated functions — Jobs on "Electrical Engineering and Marketing", Wozniak on "Electrical Engineering", Wayne on "Mechanical Engineering and Documentation" (S1P1-23). / WHY THEY DIFFER: A is about **offices**, which trade press rarely printed and this corpus does not contain; B is about **duties**, which a partnership deed records and no periodical would. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: A is a documented null with a stated coverage gap; B is one cataloguer's quotation of an unread document. / BEST-SUPPORTED: **no officer title is establishable in the Stage-1 record — which is not a finding that none existed.** A functional division of labour did exist, at the level the only 1976 paper records it. / RESIDUAL: the California SoS statement of information for the 1977 corporation is the carrier that would settle offices, and it was tried and not answered (C-07). / CONFIDENCE: High on the null as a null.

S1P1-CF-09 **Corpus provenance risk carried into the chronology.** CLAIM A: the cached 1976 issues are correctly month-dated and month confidence is High (A's batch-2 note). CLAIM B: the July file is internally impossible — it previews August and September content while narrating 30 October (S1P1-30). / WHY THEY DIFFER: A relies on Internet Archive metadata plus cross-references checked only for October–December; B is a cross-reference that fails. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: B is a specific observed contradiction; A is an assumption extended past its checks. / BEST-SUPPORTED: **October–December 1976 are safe** (independently cross-referenced); **January–September 1976 carry `issue-date: provisional pending page-sequence verification`**, and month confidence there is Medium. / RESIDUAL: whether the item bundles pages from a later issue or the OCR layer mixes them; only a scan inspection settles it (D's DG-D1). / CONFIDENCE: High that the contradiction exists, UNKNOWN as to its cause.

S1P1-CF-10 **Window labelling of the two best commercial artifacts (defect registered, not silently repaired).** CLAIM A (this part's §STAGE BOUNDARY JUSTIFICATION and S1P1-31): the adopted window closes 1977-01-03, so the April 1977 directory and the June/July 1977 order form are **post-window witnesses**. CLAIM B (records S1P1-05 and S1P1-06, written by the interrupted pass): both are described as appearing "inside the window". / WHY THEY DIFFER: the earlier records were minted before the boundary was argued and inherited an implicit window ending mid-1977. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: the documents' own cover dates are unambiguous (April, June, July 1977); the disagreement is about the boundary, not the artifacts. / BEST-SUPPORTED: **A**, because the boundary this file defends and the register's stage rows both carry 1977-01-03; the artifacts remain Tier-1 and are used in §D.3, labelled by date. / RESIDUAL: if the merge adopts a later Stage-1 end, S1P1-05 and S1P1-06 become correct as written and §STAGE BOUNDARY JUSTIFICATION must be re-opened — the choice is recorded here so the merge makes it deliberately. / CONFIDENCE: High (this is a labelling conflict resolvable by decision, not by evidence). **Action for merge: correct S1P1-05 and S1P1-06's "inside the window" wording; do not delete the records.**
