| **post-window witnesses, labelled** | BYTE April 1977 national store directory (~60 outlets, 22 jurisdictions, ~35 Byte Shop lines with named managers); Apple's own June 1977 advertisement and prepaid mail-order form; July 1977 re-run at pages 22–24 with a new reader-service number | ~35 enumerated outlets; 9-row price ladder; 1 order form | Commercial capacity *after* the window: a maker able to buy front-of-book position and ship nationwide | Nothing about Stage 1. These sit **outside** the adopted boundary (see `(Apple S1 §Boundary cost, s1_p1)` and S1P1-CF-10) and are the artifacts in tension with the word-of-mouth account — §F.4 | A2-35, A2-27, A2-28, A2-29, A2-30 | **High** (as printed) |
| **ecosystem trace, post-window** | TDL advertises "ZAPPLE BASIC … $50" and "THE ZAPPLE MONITOR" for the platform | 1 third-party product family, 3 printings May–July 1977 | Independent software/hardware vendors were attaching to the Apple name within months of the successor's launch | Anything about Stage-1 demand; and the naming collision is a caution, not a tribute | A2-70 | **High** (as printed) |

### L.1 What the ladder, taken as a whole, validates

Three propositions are supported without importing a single later fact. (i) **Technical validation preceded commercial validation, and both preceded the maker's own voice.** The machine is documented as *working* in April 1976, as *marketable* in September 1976, and as *the maker's own advertised offer* only in June 1977 — and by then the first product had already left the maker's price list (A2-72). (ii) **The strongest in-window commercial signal is indirect and it is a formula, not a number:** a dealer printing "10% discount from manufacturer's current list prices" proves a manufacturer's list circulated, and A2-23 simultaneously proves the figure is unrecoverable from these files. A validation ladder whose pivotal rung is an *absent number* should be reported that way. (iii) **Standing was conferred by a very small set of observers** — one editor, one club's newsletter, two retailers — and A2-48's warning generalises across this whole table: placement in a magazine whose editor was personally in the business being covered is a relationship datum, and this file prices it into provenance rather than counting it as corroboration.

### L.2 Signals the corpus cannot produce, and what their absence is worth

Absent and unreplaceable: any sell-through figure; any reorder signal from a named dealer (A2-62 — the corpus "names dealers who carried the product and never names an order"); any independent count behind the Faire's announced 12,800 (A2-43, a promoter's self-report); any repeat-purchase, return, warranty or cancellation record; and any negative signal about the product's reception, because the source class printed almost none. On the negative side, the two documented silences must be reported as **silences, not as findings of inactivity**: the club newsletters of 1976-12-10 and 1977-01-19 carry zero body occurrences of the name after five issues carried it across 1976 (C-20), and BYTE's 1977 Apple coverage does not resume until April. High-importance gaps created by this section are handed to the merge as `data_gaps.csv` rows (see the register block), with the follow-up tasks the method requires.

**Record-selection null (§2).** Validation in this corpus is *visible* only where a third party had both a press and a reason to print — so this ladder systematically over-represents the Bay Area hobby press and New York/California retailers, and cannot see the customer who bought quietly, the dealer who ordered and could not sell, or the buyer who returned a board. §L's shape (eleven rungs, nine of them other people's paper, zero of them the company's sales data) is that selection, not Apple's performance.

## M. NEGATIVE SIGNALS AND FAILURES

STATUS: WRITTEN 2026-09-25

**Scope and frame, stated before the evidence.** This section is written against the adopted Stage-1 window of **1975 ("late in 1975") → 1977-01-03** and its boundary-cost rule, both defended in `(Apple S1 §STAGE BOUNDARY JUSTIFICATION, s1_p1)`. Section letters, record IDs and conflict numbering continue across parts (§9.3); this part mints **S1P3-nn** as dossier-local claim keys and, for the first time in Apple's Stage 1, the canonical **`U.nnn`** conflict/null/untried anchors, mapped back to every part-local key in §U. Nothing here re-litigates what §A–§L settled: the `666.66` string is verified absent from `sources/` and stays FOUNDER CLAIM or UNKNOWN `(Apple S1 §E.4, s1_p2)`; no in-window document gives a 1976 price, order, unit count, revenue figure, supplier or founder officer title; a filing lineage is one source; a company's own retrospective recap and its reprints are one source.

**M.0 The asymmetry that governs this section, and why it is stated first.** §L counted eleven rungs of validation and found nine of them to be other people's paper and none of them the company's sales data `(Apple S1 §L, s1_p2)`. The negative side of the same corpus is thinner still, for a reason that is a property of the source class and not of the venture: **the 1976–77 hobby trade press printed almost no failure data about a company it liked.** Across the 16 cached BYTE issues and 13 cached Homebrew newsletters (`sources/ia_byte_1976/`, `ia_byte_1977/`, `ia_byte_1981/`, `sources/ia_homebrew/`; extent statement at `research/A2_periodical_archive_mine.md` A2-62, A2-76), there is no printed complaint, no returns figure, no cancellation, no warranty record, no reorder refusal and no critical review of either Apple machine inside the window. That is not evidence the venture went well. It is evidence that **the negative layer of this record is absent as a class**, and it means §M must be built from four substitute materials, each labelled for what it is:

1. **Documented terminations and exits** — things the record shows stopping, being abandoned, or being superseded.
2. **Counted silences** — negatives with an explicit denominator and an explicit statement that silence is not inactivity.
3. **Failures of this project's own evidence** — the retractions, demotions and mis-datings the dossiers and the two released parts recorded against themselves.
4. **The market's own churn denominator** — the neighbouring machines and dealers that were present in 1976 print and gone within five years, carried so the survivor's path is not read as the obvious one.

**Record-selection null (§2), §M.** What a real failing venture would have generated in 1976 and does not survive anywhere in this corpus: the order book, the invoice run, the yield and rework rate on hand-built boards, the returns and warranty pile, the bank relationship and any overdraft or refusal behind it, the dealer who ordered and could not sell, and any internal argument about the two product lines. None of these was ever printed by anybody, so §M cannot be read as a base rate for failure, and the near-absence of negatives here measures **the press's willingness to print them**, not the venture's freedom from them.

### M.1 Documented terminations and exits

| Variable | Value | Source | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| The partnership instrument of 1976-04-01 is superseded, terminated or abandoned by a document **this project has not read**, and no record anywhere searched states which | **UNKNOWN (with reason)** — the mechanics of the supersession of the 1976 agreement on 1977-01-03 are documented nowhere in `sources/` or in the dossiers | `research/C_corporate_legal_org.md` C-22; the two dated instruments themselves at C-27, C-28 | **High** that no such document surfaced; the question is registered, not answered → U.012 |
| Ronald Wayne's exit is invisible in the founding-era record | **Zero** body occurrences of the name in any in-window document; 23 hits across 16 BYTE issues and 13 newsletters, all unrelated (Fort Wayne, Wayne State, Wayne Green, Wayne Sewell, John Wayne, Dwayne Jeffries, Wayne Reitz) | C-21; A2-73; `research/D_adversarial.md`; independent re-run recorded as **a check, not a second witness** | **High** within these files / **Medium** overall (name is an OCR-mangling target, and BYTE printed no partnership news at all) → U.005 |
| Consideration for that exit | **UNKNOWN.** "$800" (Christie's lot essay, per C-28) and "$800 plus a later $1,500" (AP-20) are both retrospective vendor/embedded memoir; no withdrawal paper has been read | C-28, C-31; `research/A_chronology_feasibility.md` AP-20 | **Low**; carried inside U.005's residual, never as a metric |
| The board-only product line the partnership was formed to sell disappears from the maker's own print with **no discontinuation notice, no remaining-stock statement and no announcement** | Apple-1 offered by Apple in **no** advertisement in the cached corpus after May 1977; the maker's June and July 1977 pages sell the successor at $1,298 complete and a "board-only version … for the do-it-yourself hobbyist" at $598 | C-19; A2-72; `sources/ia_byte_1977/byte-1977-06.txt`, `byte-1977-07.txt` | **Medium-High** (absence from the maker's advertising read as withdrawal is an INFERENCE) → U.030 |
| The partnership-era name form is abandoned by the company's own print | "Apple Computer Co" (BYTE December 1976 editorial; Wozniak's own May 1977 byline) gives way to "Apple Computer Inc." in Apple's June 1977 advertisement; a third-party advertiser says "the Apple Corporation" by December 1980 | A2-74; C-04, C-06; U-A2-1/U-A2-11 in `research/conflicts.csv` | **High** (as printed) → U.013 |
| The maker's own advertising channel went unused for the whole of 1976 | Apple placed **no** advertisement of its own in any 1976 document in the corpus (AP-22, independently re-confirmed at A2-18/A2-65); its first self-published BYTE advertisement is June 1977 (C-05) | AP-22; A2-18, A2-65; C-05, C-23(i) | **Medium-High** — non-use of a channel is documented; that the non-use was a *decision* is not → U.030 |
| No 1976 document evidences an authorised Apple dealer relationship of any kind | The New York advertiser's own authorised-dealer block names "Sphere • IMSAI / Processor Technology / SWTPC 6800 & CT 1024" and **excludes** Apple; the Berkeley mail-order house claims no relationship at all | `research/B_founder_forensics.md`/s1_p1 record S1P1-17 over `sources/ia_byte_1976/byte-1976-09.txt` (l.25277 area) and `byte-1976-11.txt` | **High** for this corpus → U.008 |
| The founding period's best non-company primary goes quiet exactly across the boundary event | Homebrew `hcc0211` (1976-12-10) and `hcc0213` (1977-01-19) carry **zero** body occurrences after five consecutive 1976 issues carried the name; BYTE's 1977 Apple coverage does not resume until April | C-20; s1_p1 record S1P1-29 | **High** that the two issues are silent; expressly **not** that the company was inactive → U.021 |

**Mechanism, per §7's coda rule.** For six of these eight rows the mechanism is **UNKNOWN**, and it is printed as UNKNOWN rather than narrated. A product line vanishing from its maker's price list is consistent with exhausted components, with a deliberate pivot, with a page-layout economy, or with a stock position already sold through; the corpus contains no document that ranks these. §2 forbids the comforting reading (that Apple retired the Apple-I because the Apple-II was obviously better), and the record forbids it too: the only Apple-side statement in the vicinity is the designer's May 1977 past-tense sentence about the first machine (`sources/ia_byte_1977/byte-1977-05.txt`, A2-25), which dates a design and describes a channel, and says nothing about a decision to discontinue.

### M.2 Negatives inside the market and the channel

- **Share of shelf, measured from the only printable denominator.** At the New York retailer that had advertised the machine since September 1976, Apple occupies **one line of about twenty-five** by May 1977 (`1/25 = 4%`, A2-67; s1_p2 record S1P2-10). This is a count of printed brand lines on one page, not of revenue, units or preference; it is carried here as the single most negative-looking *quantified* in-family datum the Stage-1 record supports, and it is not evidence of failure either.
- **No reorder signal exists.** The corpus "names dealers who carried the product and never names an order" (A2-62; `Apple S1 §F.1, s1_p2`). Absence of a reorder is not evidence of no reorder; it is the documented null registered at **U.027**.
- **The pivotal validation rung is an absent number.** The strongest price evidence inside the window is a dealer's *formula* — "Starred items available by mail order at 10% discount from manufacturer's current list prices" (`sources/ia_byte_1976/byte-1976-11.txt`, A2-12) — which proves a manufacturer's list circulated and states no figure. §L.1's point is carried into §M: a validation ladder whose central rung is an absent number cannot be scored as a success or a failure on price.
- **Primacy claims in the channel were contested, and Apple was not the claimant.** Three interested parties print three different scopes of "first" (Terrell's franchise claim, Computer Mart's East-Coast claim, the club's own mail-order precedent) — U-A2-5 in `research/conflicts.csv`, minted at **U.015**. The negative content for Apple is that in 1976 print it is **the stocked object in other people's primacy stories**, never a claimant of any.
- **The Faire number is a promoter's self-report.** The announced 12,800 attendance has no independent count behind it (A2-43; `research/quantitative.csv` row for 1977-04) and the event lies after the boundary in any case; it appears here only to record that the venue in which the successor was billed is unmeasured → **U.022**.

### M.3 The market's churn denominator (so the survivor's path is not the obvious one)

A2-75 collects the dead ends that the same pages print alongside Apple, all as printed fact: a "complete microcomputer system … for $675" from an electrical-tools company (ETC-1000, Hawthorne CA, June 1976, A2-68); the GULLIVER 1 promoted by the very Skokie dealer that stocked Apple (April 1977, A2-63); Vector 1, Quantum/Seals, Tarbell, Oliver, Polymorphic Systems, Morrow, Quay Corp, Compucolor, Alpha Micro and Smoke Signal as stocked lines (A2-67); "BABY by STM Systems" named in the same technical sentence as "the new Apple computer" (A2-14); and a MITS-style line printed with the words **"(Discontinued; limited to stock on hand.)"** in September and October 1976. **Interpretive coda, with mechanism named as UNKNOWN:** the corpus's own later issues show a large part of this advertising base absent by December 1980, so the churn is a documented property of the periodical record. It is offered as the denominator against which Apple's 1976 dealer presence should be read. It is **not** evidence that Apple's product choices were superior; the mechanism that separated survivors from the listed dead ends is not in this corpus, and §2 bars the inference in both directions.

### M.4 Failures of this project's own evidence (recorded, not repaired here)

These are the negatives that any reader of this volume must know before trusting a positive. Each keeps both sides and each is anchored.

1. **A probe support that collapsed.** The chronology probe's start-edge confidence row and its fact-list #4 rest on the Homebrew newsletter of 1976-04-30 as a record of an Apple demonstration to Homebrew; `research/D_adversarial.md` L-3 establishes the paragraph sits under the standing column "NOTES FROM THE NORTH", whose subject is the **Sonoma County Micro Computer Club** meeting in Cotati — a reprinted letter from a different club. Consequence adopted in §s1_p1: the April 1976 demonstration-to-Homebrew story is **unsupported and not used**. → **U.020**
2. **A corroboration that was demoted.** AP-23's Library-of-Congress page, logged as an independent witness to the FY1978–FY1980 revenue series, is a 2023 page citing 2001–2020 retellings: **a derivative copy, not a second origin** (s1_p1 record S1P1-21). The whole printed FY1978–FY1980 series therefore rests on **one** unsigned 1981 column (`sources/ia_byte_1981/byte-1981-02.txt`). → **U.023**
3. **A count that is not a count.** "About 200 Apple-1s" traces to a wiki-derived figure whose only cited support is a 2011 Wikipedia retrieval (D L-9); the collector registry publishes *surviving* serials, which is artifact scholarship and not a production total. → **U.024**
4. **A grep recipe that contaminates its own nulls.** The probe's provenance headers now produce false positives in ten of thirteen Homebrew files, including all five issues on which AP-26's 1975 silence null rests (A2-05, A2-77). Every negative in this volume was counted with lines 1–5 excluded; a future re-run that does not will disagree with §M. → **U.025**
5. **A window-labelling defect registered by the released part.** s1_p1 records S1P1-05 and S1P1-06 describe the April 1977 directory and the June/July 1977 order form as appearing "inside the window"; under the adopted boundary they do not. The defect is registered, not silently repaired (S1P1-CF-10). → **U.019**
6. **A mis-pointed conflict key, found by this part.** s1_p1 record S1P1-10 ("a cached later column computing public-offering figures does not foot internally") points its `Conflicts:` cell at **S1P1-CF-09**, which is the corpus issue-dating conflict; the offering-arithmetic conflict its text describes is U-A2-9 in `research/conflicts.csv`. The mapping is made here on subject matter and the slip is registered rather than edited away. → **U.011** (with U.010)
7. **An unresolved internal impossibility in the corpus spine.** `sources/ia_byte_1976/byte-1976-07.txt` previews pieces appearing in "BYTE's August and September issues" while narrating events through "October 30" (D L-6; s1_p1 record S1P1-30). Nothing in the adopted boundary depends solely on that file, but every Stage-1 statement sourced to a January–September 1976 BYTE file carries `issue-date: provisional pending page-sequence verification`, and month confidence there is **Medium**, not High. → **U.018**
8. **A registry route that returned nothing and is not a null.** No 1976–77 entity record surfaced from the California SoS route (tried, unanswered: C-07) or from a "Utah filing" hypothesis (C-26). Both are **UNANSWERED/UNTRIED**, never EMPTY. → **U.042**, **U.043**

### M.5 What §M cannot show, stated as a boundary

Not a finding of this section, and not to be inferred from it: that the venture had no failures; that the first product sold or did not sell; that any dealer was satisfied or dissatisfied; that the partnership's general-liability posture was ever weighed (C-18 shows nobody writing about it, which is not the same as nobody knowing it); or that the two-week absence of the name from the club press reflects any operational event. Every one of these is a **documented null** with its failed search named in §S, and every one is anchored (**U.026**–**U.036**) so that a later pass cannot quietly convert §M's thinness into a judgment.

**CLAIM RECORDS MINTED IN §M** (dossier-local; global uniqueness lives only in `sources.csv` at merge, §13).

## N. FOUNDER DECISIONS

STATUS: WRITTEN 2026-09-25

**The evidentiary problem this section must state before it can be read.** A decision is an internal act. The Stage-1 record of Apple contains **no internal document of any kind**: no minutes, no memorandum, no board paper, no diary, no letter written inside the venture and preserved, and — because the only 1976 instruments reach this project through an auction catalogue — no clause of the one paper the founders did sign. Everything callable a "decision" below is therefore **an inference from a dated public act to an act of choosing**, and the inference is weakest exactly where the story is strongest. §10 lists "product history is vague" and "funding history is incomplete" as research-debt triggers; both fire here, and the honest output is a table whose `Rationale` column says **UNKNOWN** more often than a founder-biography table would.

**Three rules applied to every row.** (i) **Date the act, not the memory of the act**: a retrospective account is tagged `RETROSPECTIVE SOURCE` (§6) and can only establish that someone later believed a choice was made. (ii) **Alternatives must be ones the period's own print shows to have been available** — a counterfactual is only admissible if some 1976–77 document proves the option existed and was visible (that test is applied again in §O). (iii) **`Actual result` may reference post-stage outcomes only when labelled `RETROSPECTIVE`**, and where it is labelled so it does no argumentative work (§2).

### N.1 The decision register (§7 line format)

| Date | Decision | State before | Information available | Unknowns | Alternatives | Constraints | Rationale | Expected result | Actual result | Evidence | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1976-04-01 | Form as an **unincorporated partnership of three**, trading as "Apple Computer Company", rather than as a corporation | A working 6502 board designed "late in 1975" per its designer; Wozniak and Jobs employed elsewhere (HP and Atari per memoir, **undocumented in this corpus**); no entity of any kind in the record before this date | A three-page typed agreement bearing three signatures, catalogued with a 45/45/10 allocation and three functional duties ("Mechanical Engineering and Documentation", "Electrical Engineering", "Electrical Engineering and Marketing") — **text unread by this project** | Whether a corporation was considered and rejected; who drafted it; what consideration moved; whether "Apple Computer Company" was a registered business name or a trading style; place of signing beyond the catalogue's bracket "[Mountain View, California]" | A corporation (nine months later, one was formed); sole proprietorship; remaining an unincorporated arrangement with no paper at all | No capital, no premises of record, no filing; general partnership carries **unlimited** liability, which the record shows nobody writing about (C-18) | **UNKNOWN** — no document records anyone weighing corporate form against partnership; the retrospective partnership-avoidance motive is Wayne's later memory, classified FOUNDER CLAIM (retrospective) | Not stateable: no document records an expectation | **RETROSPECTIVE**: the firm did not incorporate until 1977-01-03, and the partnership style dies out of the company's own print by June 1977 (A2-74) | C-27/C-28/C-29/C-34; U.001; U.014 | **Medium** on the event, **Low** on every motive |
| 1976-04-12 | **Wayne withdraws**; the remaining two continue against a one-page "Amendment letter to the above" signed by all three | A three-partnership, eleven days old | Two separately dated instruments in the same lot, eleven days apart, the interval internally corroborated by the lot's own two dates | The consideration ($800 alone, or $800 then a later $1,500); who initiated it; whether the amendment severed liability or only interest; whether Wayne's later suit to clear his name from partnership debts is in any reachable docket (RECAP search returned nothing pre-1990) | Staying in, with a changed split; a dormant interest; refusal to sign anything | Two partners needed a third signature for whatever the amendment did; no capital was on the table in the record | **UNKNOWN** — "no regrets" is a later quotation with no documentary anchor; 12%/12-day canon is arithmetically impossible against 45/45/10 | Not stateable | **RETROSPECTIVE**: the 1981 cap table lists three 8.3M-share holders and no fourth name — an absence, not a record of termination (C-21) | C-28, C-21; U.005 | **High** on the two dates, **Low** on everything they mean |
| 1976 (whole window) | Distribute **through other people's paper**: allow dealers to advertise the machine while placing **no maker advertisement of its own** | A board with no printed price, no address of record and no letterhead | September 1976 New York retailer advertisement; November 1976 Berkeley mail-order offer at "10% discount from manufacturer's current list prices"; October–December re-runs; a November 1976 technical article listing Apple among machines "currently being marketed" | Whether any dealer held stock under agreement (no 1976 document evidences any relationship, and the New York advertiser's own authorised line excludes Apple); whether the maker *could* advertise; whether a list price was *sent* or merely quoted | Maker advertising was being done at the time by Processor Technology, IMSAI and Sphere in the same magazine; mail order was being done by the Berkeley house; franchising was the loud channel thesis of 1976–77 print (A2-66); the club bulletin board was a live free channel (A2-71) | No capital for advertising; no address or telephone to print — Apple's first address of record is Cupertino, printed May–June 1977 | **UNKNOWN.** A decision, a default, or an absence of capacity — the three are indistinguishable on this record | Not stateable | **RETROSPECTIVE**: the maker's first self-published advertisement is June 1977, one quarter after the boundary | AP-22 (re-confirmed A2-18/A2-65); A2-08, A2-12, A2-14, A2-66; s1_p1 S1P1-16/17/20; U.030 | **High** on the non-use, **UNKNOWN** on the choice |
| by 1976-11 | Maintain a **manufacturer's list price** that dealers discount against, while printing no figure in the maker's own name | Product in dealer hands | A discount formula (November 1976) that entails a circulating list; the only maker-side number anywhere near it is the designer's May 1977 rounded band "a price under $700 at the retail level" | The figure. **No 1976 document in `sources/` states any Apple price**; `666.66` is verified absent and the corpus's only dollar-shaped 666 is a competitor's anchor ("IMSAI 8080 … a $666 value / only $595") | The field's conventions were available and printed: a kit/assembled pair (Sol-20 at $995/$1,495), a single complete-system price (ETC-1000 at $675), a strike-through anchor (IMSAI) | A two-tier kit/assembled structure requires inventory and packaging the record never shows | **UNKNOWN** — the arithmetic is observable only from a dealer's formula, and the maker's own band is a rounded retrospective-in-window statement | Not stateable | **RETROSPECTIVE**: the maker's first printed ladder, June 1977, separates board from system by exactly $700 — the same rounded band the designer used for the *first* machine | A2-12; A2-23; A2-26; A2-28; s1_p2 §E.4, §E.5; U.004, U.026 | **High** that a list existed, **UNKNOWN** its amount |
| 1976-11-20 | **Show the successor to the magazine that had hosted the field**, in a motel room, and let it be seen running | The first machine is in dealer hands; the second is a prototype | The editor's own account, published April 1977: "I first saw the Apple-ll on November 20 1976 when Stephen Wozniak and Stephen Jobs stopped by a motel room in Palo Alto", with a same-evening BASIC session | Whether anything was agreed that night; whether the machine shown was the one advertised in June 1977; whether publication was discussed at all | Not demonstrating; demonstrating to a different title; waiting for the Faire | Single witness; he was a friend of the subjects, and BYTE was itself commercially engaged in the category (four issues of Byte Shop vendor advertising before Apple's first print) | **UNKNOWN** — the record shows an act of disclosure, not a reason for it | Not stateable | **RETROSPECTIVE**: the same title prints the designer's bylined system description in May 1977 and the maker's first advertisement in June 1977 | A2-31, A2-59 (`sources/ia_byte_1977/byte-1977-04.txt`); `Apple S1 §G.5, s1_p2` | **Medium-High** on the event; **UNKNOWN** on the decision |
| 1976-12 | Commit money and calendar to a **public trade appearance**: "Apple Computers" appears on the committed exhibitor list for the first West Coast Computer Faire | No maker print, no address, no telephone in 1976 | The December 1976 exhibitor list in BYTE and the club's own list; the Faire billed for April 15–17, 1977 at the San Francisco Civic Auditorium (1976-12-10 and 1977-02-16 printings) | Booth size, cost, what was shown, whether the booth materialised; the schedule conflict between the billed three days and a correspondent's two | Absentee distribution through dealers cost nothing and required no commitment | A committed exhibitor slot is an unpaid-or-paid obligation in print; the venue's ~200 billed exhibits is a promoter's figure | **UNKNOWN** — but the act itself is the only **forward-looking organisational act visible in 1976 print**, and that is why it is in the register | A public showing among peers | **RETROSPECTIVE**: billed attendance 12,800, a promoter's self-report with no independent count | A2-16, A2-41, A2-43, A2-44; `research/timeline.csv` rows 1976-12, 1976-12-10; U.016, U.022 | **High** that the name was on the list; **Low** on what was decided behind it |
| 1977-01-03 | **Incorporate in California** as Apple Computer, Inc. | A two-man partnership trading under at least two printed name forms, selling through other people's advertisements, with no maker print | One registrant sentence in the FY1994 Form 10-K, read at line level; independently, "Apple, incorporated in 1977" in an unsigned 1981 trade column — **two documents of unrelated lineage agreeing on the year** | Officers, purpose, authorised capital, share structure at formation, and the mechanics by which the 1976 papers were superseded — **none** documented anywhere searched; the California SoS route returned nothing and is UNANSWERED, not empty | Continuing as a partnership (nine months of it produced no filing and no visible harm); forming an entity in another state (a "Utah filing" hypothesis was tried and produced nothing, C-26) | Nothing in the record shows outside capital arriving *before* the date, which is what a "capital forced incorporation" story would need; Markkula's arrival is undated in every source this project holds | **UNKNOWN.** Liability, capital, tax or credibility may each have motivated it; no document ranks them | Not stateable | **RETROSPECTIVE**: the corporation files no reaching EDGAR text before 1994-01-26, so the whole early corporate record is print-only | s1_p1 S1P1-12, S1P1-13 (from FY1994 10-K l.132–133 and `byte-1981-02.txt` l.48497–48498); C-01, C-07, C-26 | **High** on the year, **Medium** on the day, **UNKNOWN** on the why |
| undated in this record | Accept **outside money and a third name** | A corporation nine days to nine months old, depending on the date assumed | Exactly one in-record sentence: "A C Markkula, 32 years old, who took Apple from a garage operation to its current enviable position, also holds 8.3 million shares" (February 1981) — his **only** occurrence in ~13 MB | Whether the $91,000 guarantee, ~$250,000, or a 26–28% stake appear in **any** retrieved document: they do not. His arrival date appears in no retrieved document either | Self-financing from dealer receipts (the record cannot price these); a bank loan (Wayne's retrospective $15,000 belongs to U.006's conflict, not to a document) | No 1976–77 document shows the firm seeking capital | **UNKNOWN**; the "obvious necessity of outside capital" reading is hindsight and is refused | Not stateable | **RETROSPECTIVE**: by 1980-12 three named holders each hold 8.3M shares on the printed cap table | C-11, A2-51, A2-61a; s1_p1 S1P1-24; U.007 | **High** that no document carries the terms; **UNKNOWN** on the terms |

**N.2 What the table's shape says.** Eight rows; **five rationales UNKNOWN**; two "decisions" that a biography would narrate as strategic (taking money, incorporating) are, on this record, **dated acts with no recoverable motive**; and one row — the 1976 non-advertising posture — is not clearly a decision at all but possibly an incapacity. That distribution is the finding of §N, and it is the opposite of the received Apple founding story's usual form, in which every step is a choice made for a reason. **Mechanism:** no document in the corpus records deliberation of any kind; the record-selection null (§2) applies with full force — deliberation is precisely the material that does not survive a survivor's archive. **Alternative explanation, tested and not excluded:** that deliberation was recorded and has simply not been digitised (the partnership papers exist and are unread; a charter file probably exists and was not reached). Under that explanation §N's blanks are retrieval failures, not historical ones, and the named routes are §S's U.037–U.054. **Confidence:** High for the table as a description of the record; no confidence is claimed for it as a description of the founders' minds.

**CLAIM RECORDS MINTED IN §N.**

## O. COUNTERFACTUAL OPPORTUNITIES

STATUS: WRITTEN 2026-09-25

**Admission test, stated first because this section is where hindsight does its easiest damage.** §2 forbids treating the eventual outcome as evidence that any 1976 choice was right, and it equally forbids the mirror-image error — treating the outcome as evidence that a road not taken was a *missed* one. So a counterfactual is admitted to §O only if **a document dated inside or adjacent to the window proves the option existed and was visible in the market**, and each row must then carry what the record cannot supply: whether anyone in the venture considered it (never, on this corpus), and what its effect would have been (never). Where an option is famous in retrospect but absent from 1976–77 print, it is refused here and named as refused. **The anti-hagiography test applied to every row: would this paragraph read the same if the firm had failed in 1981?** Rows that would not are marked and demoted.

### O.1 Options the period's print proves existed, and that the Stage-1 record does not show the venture taking

| # | Option, as evidenced by period print | Who is documented using it in 1976–77 | What Apple's record shows | What the record cannot show | Class / Conf |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| O-a | **Maker advertising in the trade press.** Sol-20, IMSAI, Sphere and Processor Technology buy pages in BYTE throughout 1976–77, with printed kit/assembled price pairs (A2-45, A2-67, A2-68) | Competing makers, repeatedly, in the same magazine that carried Apple's first print | **No** Apple-placed advertisement in any 1976 document (AP-22; re-confirmed A2-18/A2-65); the maker's first page is June 1977 | Cost, whether it was considered, whether capacity to pay existed; **and whether non-advertising cost anything** — no dealer complains in print either way | FACT (the non-use) / **UNKNOWN** (the trade-off). **Medium-High** |
| O-b | **Direct mail order to strangers on standard hobby terms** — 10% off list, 2% carriage, $80 minimum, postage-paid orders, bank-card acceptance appearing in the field by 1977 | Kentucky Fried Computers, Berkeley, from at least its June 1976 club notice; Apple's own form by June–July 1977 | By November 1976 a *third party* is offering the Apple-1 by mail order on exactly these terms; Apple's own mail order is post-window | Whether Apple could have mailed against a list in 1976 (no address of record and no telephone is printed until May–June 1977) — this row is **the strongest capacity argument in §O**, and it is still an inference | INFERENCE. **Medium** |
| O-c | **A dealer network as an institution**: franchising is the loud channel thesis of the period's print — Computer Shack's "double or quadruple" franchise plan, Radio Shack franchisees shopping the category, and Terrell printed as founder of "the world's first computer store franchise" (A2-66, A2-07) | Third parties, loudly, in 1976–77 | Apple appears in dealers' ads but **no 1976 document evidences an authorised-dealer relationship of any kind**, and the New York advertiser's own authorised-dealer block omits Apple (s1_p1 S1P1-17) | Whether a network was offered, declined or discussed; whether the April 1977 ~35-store directory describes Apple's channel or only retail's — §F/§D.3 already hold this tension open (U.008) | FACT (the absence) / UNKNOWN (the reason). **High / UNKNOWN** |
| O-d | **The club channel, free of charge**: the Homebrew "BULLETIN BOARD" carries hardware sales and used-machine asks in print (A2-71), and the club's own survey counts 101 live systems and ~250 attendees at one 1976-06-09 meeting (A2-61) | Club members, individually | The machine is driven to a **different** club's meeting and shown there (1976-04-30 letter, A2-19/A2-20); one user letter in the club's own pages shows a modified TV producing printout from "the Apple Computer" (A2-22) | Whether the founders ever posted, offered or sold through the bulletin board; the "immediate orders at Homebrew" story has **no printed footprint at all** in the run this project holds (A2-76) | CONTEMPORARY OBSERVATION / **UNKNOWN**. **Medium** |
| O-e | **A two-tier kit/assembled price structure**, printed by competitors as a matter of course ($995 kit / $1,495 assembled, Sol-20; Apple's own later $598 board / $1,298 system) | Processor Technology, 1976-11 | The first machine is described by its designer as sold "as a completely assembled and tested processor board" with a rounded band "under $700 at the retail level" (May 1977) — no kit tier is printed by anyone for it | Whether a kit version was considered, priced, refused; whether components allowed one. **The record is silent, and silence here is not a refusal** | FOUNDER CLAIM (contemporaneous) for the band; **UNKNOWN** for the option |
| O-f | **Defensive trade-mark posture.** By December 1980 a third-party advertiser prints "trademarks of the Apple Corporation", and the recorded-name conflict with the record company has its own later history (C-16, C-17) | Advertisers asserting marks generically | **No** Apple-owned trade-mark, registration or notice line appears in any in-window document; the earliest Apple-assigned patent application is 1977-04-11, post-window, and names the **corporation**, never the partnership (C-35) | Whether any name review happened; C-17 records that the record-company dispute "leaves no trace" in-window, and the 2003 docket is firewall-tagged and is not evidence about 1976 | FACT (as printed) / **UNKNOWN** (posture). **Medium** |
| O-g | **Staying a partnership, or incorporating earlier.** Two dated legal events, nine months apart, prove both states were live options in a material sense | The firm itself, in sequence | The 1977-01-03 incorporation is the window's hardest date, on two documents of unrelated lineage | Whether the interval was chosen, forced, or simply how long it took; **succession mechanics are documented nowhere** (C-22, U.012) | FACT (both dates) / UNKNOWN (the interval's meaning). **High / UNKNOWN** |
| O-h | **Taking, or not taking, outside money** | Markkula's later holding is printed once, in 1981 | No in-window document shows the firm seeking or receiving capital; "Markkula" occurs **zero** times in any file dated 1975 through December 1980 | His entry date, his terms, and whether the company needed him — three separate nulls, all carried as U.007 and U.028 | **UNKNOWN** throughout. **High** that no document carries the terms |

### O.2 Options the record shows were *not* visible, and which are therefore refused here

- **"The obvious retail chain" and "the obvious bank line."** No 1976–77 document in any cached family shows Apple at a bank, on a credit line, or in a lease. The two retrospective capital figures in circulation — Wayne's $15,000 loan and the $1,000 raised by selling a calculator and a vehicle — conflict with each other and anchor to no document (U.006); they belong to §K and §U, not to a counterfactual about financing strategy. **Refused.**
- **"Contract manufacturing at scale."** The corpus prints no supplier, no procurement, no payroll, no yield and no production site (S1P2-11; §G). Any sentence beginning "had they industrialised production earlier…" has no antecedent in the evidence and is excluded from this file. **Refused.**
- **"The obvious mass market."** §H's in-period knowability finding is that an observer could count clubs, dealer pages and one magazine's circulation, and could not size a national market; a counterfactual that imports the 1977–82 consumer curve into 1976 is hindsight contamination by definition. **Refused.**
- **"Keeping Ronald Wayne."** Tempting and inadmissible: nothing in the record shows what he would have done with a continuing 10%, and the only dated facts about him are two signatures eleven days apart. His 2022 written account is a FOUNDER CLAIM (retrospective) and is used in §M and §K only. **Refused as a counterfactual; carried as a null (U.029).**
- **A firewall demotion, made explicitly.** Row O-f's later history and row O-b's post-window success are both **far-side** knowledge. They are printed because the method requires the alternative set to come from period print, not because they were opportunities the window could see; the anti-hagiography test would fail any paragraph that said otherwise.

### O.3 What §O adds to the volume

Two things, and no more. First, a **capacity argument**: the strongest counterfactual the record actually supports is O-b — that the mail-order mechanism existed, was printed, and was used by a third party to sell Apple's machine in November 1976 before the maker had any printed address or telephone. That is an observation about what was *possible* in the window, not about what was *wise*. Second, a **denominator argument**: O-a, O-c and O-e are all options that the corpus's churn inventory (§M.3) proves were being taken by firms that were gone within five years, so the mere availability of an option is weak evidence that declining it was a mistake. **Mechanism** for why these alternatives existed and were visible: a single trade magazine with an advertising-driven business model, a club press with no editorial distance, and a component market whose prices were public. **Alternative explanation, not excluded:** that the founders' real constraint was hours and hands, not options — for which the corpus has exactly one datum, Sphere printed in 1976 as "all the company consisted of was two people in a garage" (about **another** firm, L-5), and none for Apple. **Confidence:** Medium for the admission test's outcomes; **UNKNOWN** for every counterfactual's effect. §O makes no claim that any of these rows would have changed anything, which is the whole point of the firewall.

**CLAIM RECORDS MINTED IN §O.**

S1P3-08 Claim: The Stage-1 record supports a bounded set of live alternatives, all of them taken from period print by third parties, and supports no statement about whether the founders saw them. — Date: 1976-06 → 1977-06 — Source: A2-07, A2-12, A2-39, A2-45, A2-61, A2-66, A2-67, A2-68, A2-71; `research/C_corporate_legal_org.md` C-16/C-17/C-35 — Source date: 1976-06 → 1980-12 — URL: https://archive.org/details/byte-magazine-1976-11 ; …/hcc0206 ; …/byte-magazine-1977-04 — Archived: `sources/ia_byte_1976/`, `ia_byte_1977/`, `ia_homebrew/` — Tier: 1 — Class: FACT (that the options were printed by others) / **UNKNOWN** (their visibility to, and value for, Apple) — Passage: "all the company consisted of was two people in a garage" (of Sphere, 1976-07, under the issue-date caveat at U.018) — Conf: High for existence of the options, **no confidence claimed** for their assessment — Corroboration: many unrelated advertisers — Conflicts: U.008, U.006, U.029

S1P3-09 Claim: The one counterfactual with real documentary support is the mail-order channel, because a third party used it to sell Apple's machine in November 1976 while the maker still had no printed address or telephone. — Date: 1976-11 — Source: Kentucky Fried Computers advertisement, BYTE November 1976 (A2-12, A2-40), against C-05's May–June 1977 first address of record — Source date: 1976-11 — URL: https://archive.org/details/byte-magazine-1976-11 — Archived: `sources/ia_byte_1976/byte-1976-11.txt` — Tier: 1 — Class: **INFERENCE** (from two printed facts) — Passage: "Starred items available by mail order at 10% discount from manufacturer's current list prices." — Conf: Medium — Corroboration: 1 advertiser's offer + 1 dated absence; `independence_note`: the Berkeley house's June 1976 club notice (A2-39) prints the same formula for another line — same firm, so **one** origin extended in time, not two origins — Conflicts: none; bears on U.026, U.030

## P. QUANTITATIVE METRICS TABLE

STATUS: WRITTEN 2026-09-25

**Reading rules for this table.** (§8) Every value carries its source cell and its confidence cell; `UNKNOWN` is a complete value and appears here as often as any number, because **the money layer of Apple's Stage 1 is a null and a metrics table that hid the null would be the volume's single largest misstatement**. (§7) Derived figures carry their arithmetic in the table and are labelled `DERIVED`, never presented as observed. (§6) All currency is nominal 1976–77 dollars; units, boards, systems, dealers, lines and dollars are distinguished; no inflation adjustment is applied anywhere. (§3) A figure carried by several printings of one campaign, or by several documents of one filing lineage, is **one** source. Rows marked **[reg]** already exist in `research/quantitative.csv` or were requested by `_parts/s1_p2.md`; rows marked **[new]** are requested in this part's register block at the end of the file.

**P.1 Not computed, and why (the list is part of the deliverable).** No growth rate, revenue multiple, margin or per-unit economics may be computed **for the Stage-1 window**, because FY1976 and FY1977 revenue is `UNKNOWN` (P13) and no unit count exists (P12); a "revenue per employee", "burn", "runway" or "customer-acquisition" figure is impossible on this record and no proxy is offered. No price-elasticity or take-rate statement may be made from P9/P10, which describe one advertiser's terms for a machine whose list price is unrecovered. The FY1978–FY1980 series in P14–P19 must not be back-projected to characterize 1976–77, and P16–P19 describe an investor-relations recap printed four years after the window. The Faire attendance figure (A2-43) is a promoter's self-report with no independent denominator and is therefore not used as a demand metric anywhere in this volume.

**P.2 Register reconciliation.** This part requests **no change** to any existing `research/quantitative.csv` row, and requests the eight new metric rows printed in the register block (P2, P4, P20, P26, P27, P28, P29, P32). Where a metric here duplicates a row s1_p2 already requested (P10, P23, P24), the merge should keep the s1_p2 row and discard the duplicate; the anchor keys, not the numbers, decide.

**Q.1 Three things the spine shows that no summary of it should be allowed to hide.** (i) **The window's densest months are the emptiest.** November 1976 through January 1977 holds one friendly sighting, one exhibitor list, two silent newsletters and then the incorporation itself — the boundary event is invisible in the very press that had tracked the firm monthly, and reaches us through a 1994 filing sentence and a 1981 clause (U.021). (ii) **Every dated act is somebody else's paper until June 1977.** Counting from the start edge, the first fifteen dated Apple-bearing documents are a club letter, two dealers' advertisements, an editorial clause, a technical article, a show-floor note and an exhibitor list; the maker's own voice is not in the record until after the window closes (U.020). (iii) **The two most load-bearing numbers in the whole period — a price and an order — enter the record only as claims with no in-window carrier**, and the spine's only monetary rows for 1976 are a competitor's $675, a dealer's discount formula and a calculator's street price.

**CLAIM RECORDS MINTED IN §Q.**

S1P3-11 Claim: Two 1976 instruments eleven days apart, both signed by three named men, are the only dated first-party artifacts of the partnership, and neither has been read by this project. — Date: 1976-04-01, 1976-04-12 — Source: `research/C_corporate_legal_org.md` C-27, C-28, C-31, C-34 (Christie's lot 242; custody via Sotheby's New York 2011-12-13 lot 241) — Source date: 2026-01-23 (catalogue) — URL: UNKNOWN to this part (recorded in C §7) — Archived: `research/C_corporate_legal_org.md` §7; **no bytes of the instrument are in `sources/`** — Tier: 3 catalogue describing a Tier-1 object — Class: FACT (existence, dates, signatures as catalogued) / **UNKNOWN** (clauses, consideration) — Passage: "Typed document signed … 1 April 1976. Three pages, letter-sized paper. With: Amendment letter to the above. … 12 April 1976. One page, letter-sized paper." — Conf: High (existence) / Medium (allocation) / Low ($800) — Corroboration: **1 object**; the second auction event is the same paper, **not a second witness** — Conflicts: U.005, U.012

## R. END-OF-STAGE STRUCTURED SNAPSHOT (window 1975 → 1977-01-03)

STATUS: WRITTEN 2026-09-25

**What this table is for.** A snapshot of the venture's observable state **at the boundary date**, with no later fact imported and no unknown filled by inference. Each cell carries its source and its confidence (§8); `UNKNOWN` means the Stage-1 record does not establish it, and never means the thing did not happen. Where the standard §R frame (users/customers, capacity, unit economics) does not fit a two-machine hardware start with no filed accounts, the adapted equivalent is given with a note on why (§7).

### R.1 Entity, law and property

| Variable | Value | Source | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Legal form at the boundary | **Corporation** from 1977-01-03 (California); before that an unincorporated partnership trading as "Apple Computer Company", with **no filed formation record located** | FY1994 10-K l.132–133; C-01, C-07 | High (form change) / **UNKNOWN** (succession mechanics) → U.012 |
| Dated instruments of the prior form | 1976-04-01 (three pages, three signatures) and 1976-04-12 (one page, three signatures); **text unread by this project** | C-27, C-28 | High (existence/dates) / Medium (contents) → U.005 |
| Officers and governance | **No officer title for any principal is documented in the cached corpus**; the founding instrument allocated *functions*, not offices, per the catalogue's reading | C-13; C-29 | High (the census) / **Medium** (the functions) → U.014 |
| Name as printed in 1976–early 1977 | "Apple Computer Co", "Apple Computers"; "Apple Computer Inc." appears **in no 1976 document**, entering in the company's own print only June 1977 | C-04, C-05; A2-74 | High → U.013 |
| Premises | **UNKNOWN.** The first address of record (20863 Stevens Creek Blvd Bldg B3-C, Cupertino) is printed May–June **1977**; "Los Altos" traces to a Wikipedia raw page; the catalogue's own bracket for the signing is "[Mountain View, California]"; the garage motif is a 1976 genre phrase printed about another firm | C-11, C-34; A2-25; D L-5 | **UNKNOWN**, with three competing place labels each priced → U.002 |
| Registered IP | **None in-window.** Earliest Apple-assigned US application 1977-04-11 (Wozniak), naming the corporation; the first product was never patented | C-35; S1P2-12 | Medium-High (non-exhaustive register read) → U.034 |
| Liability posture | Corporation inside California corporate law from the date; the partnership's general-liability character is asserted only by a later participant memory, and **no 1976 document asserts limited liability for the firm** | C-18; AP-29 | High (corporate side) / Low-Medium (partnership side) → U.035 |

### R.2 Product, market and channel

| Variable | Value | Source | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|

### R.3 Money, and the shape of the null

| Variable | Value | Source | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1976–77 revenue, profit, units, order book, payroll, bank position | **UNKNOWN — none established by any document in any family searched** (periodicals, EDGAR, collector registry, court index, club press) | A2-23, A2-62, DG-1, DG-2; D L-11 | **High that it is undocumented**; the paper-only 1980 registration statement is the only plausible carrier and is unreached → U.009, U.026, U.041 |
| Founding capital | **Contested and undocumented**: a $15,000 loan (one partner's later account) against ~$1,000 raised by selling a calculator and a vehicle (a different later account); contemporaneous print supplies only a *plausibility bound* on the $500 calculator figure (`500/795 = 62.9%` vs the club's own used-market ask at `2000/3066.58 = 65.2%`) | AP-29, AP-32; A2-71 | Low–Medium as memory; **UNKNOWN** as fact → U.006 |
| Outside capital at the boundary | **UNKNOWN.** No in-window document shows the firm seeking or receiving investment; Markkula's terms appear in no retrieved document and his arrival is undated in every source held | C-10, C-11; S1P1-24 | High (the null) → U.007, U.028 |
| Earliest printed fiscal year anywhere in the corpus | **FY1978** — sales $7.8M, profit $793,497 — in an unsigned February 1981 column, on **one** witness, four years after the boundary | A2-49 | High as printed; `RETROSPECTIVE SOURCE`; the 1923-era Library-of-Congress "second witness" was demoted as derivative → U.023 |
| Unit economics (cost per board, margin, yield, rework, returns) | **NOT COMPUTABLE on this record.** No cost, no count, no sale document; no proxy offered | §P.1 | High (that it is impossible here) → U.026, U.041 |

### R.4 Operating method at the boundary (the adapted "current strategy" row)

What the dated record shows the venture *doing* as it crosses 1977-01-03, with nothing teleological: **design in the garage-and-club milieu, prove the machine to third parties who then advertise it, print no maker-side price or address, let other people's paper be the storefront, sign partnership papers that no archive has since read, and incorporate on a January date that the founding period's own press never mentions.** Six clauses; each rests on the rows above; none requires the company to have survived. **Anti-hagiography check:** every clause is equally a description of a small, under-capitalised, distribution-less firm — the form the record actually supports.

**R.5 Knowability (§7).** `KNOWABLE` in-window: that the machine existed and was named by outsiders; that a dealer circuit printed it; that two 1976 instruments and one 1977 incorporation are dated; that the maker printed nothing of its own. `NOT KNOWABLE` even now: price, orders, units, revenue, costs, capital, officers, premises, and whether anyone considered the §O alternatives. `UNKNOWN`: everything in R.3 except the FY1978 row, plus the succession mechanics and Wayne's consideration.

**CLAIM RECORDS MINTED IN §R.**

S1P3-12 Claim: At the boundary date the venture's observable state is fully describable in six clauses, and every monetary and organisational variable in it is a documented null rather than an estimate. — Date: 1977-01-03 — Source: R.1–R.4 above, over `research/C_corporate_legal_org.md`, `research/A2_periodical_archive_mine.md` and the two released parts — Source date: 2026-09-26 — URL: NONE — Archived: `sources/`, `research/` — Tier: 1/3 as per row — Class: FACT (observable state) / **UNKNOWN** (unobservable state) — Passage: NO_VERBATIM_PASSAGE_RECORDED — Conf: High for the description as a description of the record — Corroboration: per row — Conflicts: U.001, U.002, U.012, U.013, U.014, U.026

## S. DATA GAPS

STATUS: WRITTEN 2026-09-25

**Three states kept distinct, and never collapsed into one another (§12, §14).**
**EMPTY** = the record contains nothing on the question and no document was found that could hold it.
**UNANSWERED** = a retrieval was attempted against this corpus or this archive and failed, timed out, or returned a non-null that does not settle the question — **a 403, 429 or 504 is a failure, not a null.**
**UNTRIED** = a named retrieval has not been attempted by any agent in this stage, and its exact query is printed here so the next pass can run it without re-deriving the need.
**No row below may be reported as "the evidence does not exist" unless it is marked EMPTY, and only EMPTY rows survive as findings about the world.** This part made **zero web requests**; every UNTRIED row carries the query that was never sent. The wording rule enforced throughout §S–§U is the one the released parts adopted: a fact is **"not establishable in the Stage-1 record"**, never "never happened".

### S.2 Three things that would be gaps in another volume and are NOT gaps here

(i) **The FY1978–FY1980 series is evidenced and deliberately excluded from Stage-1 state** — it is an investor-relations recap four years after the boundary, carried at §P14–P19 as a labelled far-side witness and nowhere as window performance (§6, §2). (ii) **The Apple II's own launch** (billed at the April 1977 Faire, advertised June 1977) is outside the window by decision, not by absence of evidence; §E carries it as a post-window witness and U.019 registers the labelling conflict rather than smoothing it. (iii) **The 1980 offering terms** (P17, P18) are fully printed and fully post-stage; they are recorded because the *same* 1981 column is the corpus's only fiscal witness, and a reader must be able to see how far that witness stands from the window.

### S.3 Where this section's nulls came from — the searches that failed, named

Every EMPTY above was produced by a counted search, not by impression: an exhaustive token pass over the twelve cached 1976 BYTE issues and the four cached 1977 issues with provenance headers excluded (AP-22; A2-18/A2-65 re-confirmation; the false-positive mechanism documented at A2-05/A2-77); a body-only name-and-title census across 16 BYTE issues and 13 newsletters (C-13, C-21, A2-52); an EDGAR full-text search set whose floor for this registrant is 1994-01-26 (`sources/EDGAR_submissions_CIK0000320193*.json`, `sources/probe_edgar_fts_*.json`, including a pre-1994 probe that returned nothing and a control that returned hits, so the null is the archive's and not the query's); the collector-registry pages under `sources/`; and a court-index search (C-06) whose earliest relevant docket is 2003-10-08. **Where a route returned an error rather than nothing, §S records UNANSWERED, never EMPTY**: the artnet/Christie's **403**, the Santa Clara County **403/000**, the California SoS API non-response (C-07), and the Library-of-Congress demotion, which is a *provenance* failure not a retrieval failure (U.023).

**CLAIM RECORDS MINTED IN §S.**

## T. SOURCE / PROVENANCE TABLE

STATUS: WRITTEN 2026-09-25

**Keyed to existing register ids, which are re-used and never re-defined (§9.4: `sources.csv` is global-append-only per company).** Apple's Stage-1 register today carries **A2S-01 … A2S-13** (from `research/A2_periodical_archive_mine.md`); dossier C proposed a **CS-01 … CS-07** block and `_parts/s1_p2.md` proposed **S1P2-S01 … S1P2-S05**; all three blocks are provisional and **the canonical `source_id` values are assigned centrally at merge (§13)**. This part defines no global id; it proposes **S1P3-S01 … S1P3-S05** locally for documents no existing block covers, and its rows appear in the register block.

**THE LINEAGE COLUMN IS THE LOAD-BEARING ONE.** Two lineage rules govern every count in this volume. (1) **Filing lineage:** the corporation's earliest electronic text is the accession filed **1994-12-13 for FY1994**, and `sources/EDGAR_submissions_CIK0000320193.json` shows the series beginning **1994-01-26** — so *everything* about 1976–77 in this file is print, and no EDGAR document can corroborate another EDGAR document for the founding period. Any later Apple annual report restating "incorporated … January 3, 1977" is the **same corporate record**, one source however many filings. (2) **Self-recap lineage:** a company's own retrospective recapitalisation of its beginning, and every page that reprints it, is **one source** — which is what demotes AP-23's Library-of-Congress page (U.023) and what keeps the Christie's essay, the Sotheby's 2011 lot and the RR Auction pages from being three witnesses to the founding transaction (U.003). A third rule, specific to this corpus: **a dealer advertisement that re-runs across months is one campaign with a duration**, not several corroborations (applied to the New York Sept–Dec 1976 pages and Apple's own June–July 1977 pages).

**T.1 What the table cannot give this volume.** No document in it is internal to the venture except the two unread 1976 instruments; no document dates Markkula or Wayne's consideration; no document prints a 1976 Apple price; and no second origin stands behind the FY1978–FY1980 numbers. **Independence verdict for the whole stage: the window's evidence is overwhelmingly third-party paper about a company that had not yet acquired a voice of its own** — which is a stronger evidentiary position than a memoir-only start-up, and a much weaker one than a firm that filed papers. That asymmetry, not the missing $666.66, is the provenance finding of Apple's Stage 1.

**CLAIM RECORDS MINTED IN §T.**

STATUS: WRITTEN 2026-09-25

### U.i Numbering, invariants, and the mapping this section exists to make

**Apple's Stage 1 has never before carried a canonical `U.nnn` spine.** The dossiers minted **dossier-local** conflict keys (`U-AP-1…4`, `U-A2-1…13`, `U-C-1…6` — note the **hyphen**, which the anchor gate does not read as a `U.nnn` token), and the two released parts deliberately minted **no** `U.nn` anchors at all, using part-local namespaces instead (`S1P1-CF-01…10`, `S1P2-CF-01…05`) precisely so an unmerged row would not trip the parity gate (`_parts/s1_p2.md`, register-block preamble). This section mints the canonical series and maps every part-local key onto it, so the merge has **one addressable set**. `research/conflicts.csv` already carries thirteen rows under `U-A2-*`; those rows are re-keyed here, and the dossier keys survive only as alias cells.

**Six rules, binding on this section and on the merge.**
(1) **Append-only.** Anchors are minted continuously from the next free number; nothing is inserted, renumbered or "cleaned". A topic discovered late gets the next number, which is why the sales-tax conflict is **U.055** and not U.012 (the precedent is Amazon's U.112/U.113, added by an audit pass without disturbing the spine).
(2) **1:1 with register rows.** Every anchor minted here has exactly one requested row: `conflicts.csv` for **U.001–U.024** and **U.055**, `data_gaps.csv` for the nulls **U.025–U.036** and the routes **U.037–U.054**. Block count equals requested-row count; if the merge applies fewer rows than this section declares, the anchor-parity gate is *correctly* red.
(3) **Nothing is reconciled silently.** Every conflict keeps both sides. A conflict settled on arithmetic rather than on evidence says so and names what remains open; a conflict the record cannot settle stays open, because **an unresolved conflict in the record is a finding, not an embarrassment**.
(4) **Status vocabulary is not optional.** A 403/429/504 or an uncalled route is **UNANSWERED/UNTRIED**, never a null. A fact is "**not establishable in the Stage-1 record**", never "never happened".
(5) **No averaging.** Where two figures conflict, no mean, no "approximately", and no blended range may be printed anywhere in the dataset. Both values stand, each with its carrier.
(6) **One source per lineage.** A filing lineage is one source; a company's own retrospective recap and its reprints are one source; a campaign re-run is one source with a duration.

**Mapping table — part-local and dossier-local keys → canonical anchors.** Apply at merge; do not renumber the parts' records.
