*Keys deliberately **not** promoted to anchors, with the reason:* `U-A2-10`'s "1981 recap names Jobs, Wozniak and Markkula and no fourth name" is the **Wayne-absence leg of U.005/U.007** and not an independent conflict; `AP-04/AP-19` are carriers of U.001, not conflicts; `S1P1-CF-02` (which event is the Stage-1 boundary) is **resolved by decision, not by evidence**, and lives in §STAGE BOUNDARY JUSTIFICATION `(s1_p1)` with its cost registered at U.019 — re-minting it as an open anchor would re-open a boundary the released parts have already defended.

### U.ii Live conflicts

**U.001 — WHICH DATES ARE THE COMPANY'S.** CLAIM A: Apple Computer, Inc. was incorporated under the laws of the State of California on **1977-01-03** (FY1994 10-K l.132–133; the year alone in an unsigned February 1981 column — two documents of unrelated lineage). CLAIM B: a three-page partnership agreement dated **1976-04-01** exists, signed by three named men (Christie's lot 242 as catalogued); and the universe register's single cell reads "1976-04-01, Los Altos", tracing to a Wikipedia raw page. A third variant, "incorporated in **1978**", is carried by an unread carrier in s1_p1 record S1P1-02. / WHY THEY DIFFER: two legal events of different character, nine months apart, plus an unsourced place, collapsed into the single word "founded". / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: A is Tier-1 on two origins for the year, one for the day; B's existence is Tier-1 in kind but reaches us through a Tier-3 catalogue; the Los Altos cell is derivative and untraceable. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **partnership 1976-04-01, corporation 1977-01-03, two different events**; the word "founded" is not usable without attaching one of the dates. / RESIDUAL UNCERTAINTY: the day rests on one filing; the 1978 variant is unrebutted only because its carrier is unread; succession mechanics → **U.012**. / CONFIDENCE: High (year), Medium (day), Low (the 1978 leg). / Maps: U-AP-1, U-C-1, S1P1-CF-01, S1P1-02.

**U.002 — WHERE IT BEGAN, AND WHETHER THE GARAGE IS EVIDENCE.** CLAIM A: formed at **Los Altos, California** (universe register; encyclopaedic repetition), the venture operated from **a garage**. CLAIM B: the lot's own bibliographic line brackets "**[Mountain View, California]**"; every 1976 dated document places the firm nowhere; the first address of record is **Cupertino, 20863 Stevens Creek Blvd Bldg B3-C**, printed May–June **1977**; and BYTE had printed "all the company consisted of was two people in a garage" in July 1976 about **Sphere** (L-5). / WHY THEY DIFFER: A is a retrospective motif with no in-window document; B is what the dated print supports. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: three unrelated carriers for B's separate sentences; A has no documentary carrier in this corpus. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **premises UNKNOWN at every 1976 date**; the garage is demonstrably a 1976 genre phrase, which does not prove Apple had no garage, and cannot prove it had one. / RESIDUAL UNCERTAINTY: county assessor/recorder records → U.043. / CONFIDENCE: High that the motif is unanchored; Medium on the Mountain View bracket (a catalogue inference); UNKNOWN on premises. / Maps: U-A2-13, S1P1-CF-01 place leg, D L-5, DG-D5.

**U.003 — THE FIRST ORDER.** CLAIM A (canon): Paul Terrell's Byte Shop ordered **50 boards at $500** in **July 1976**, restated as fact by a 2026 auction essay. CLAIM B (record): **no 1976 document names an order**, a quantity, a buyer or a price; the Byte Shop's own 1976 advertising never mentions Apple; the corpus "names dealers who carried the product and never names an order" (A2-62). / WHY THEY DIFFER: one memorable transaction transmits better than a diffuse dealer ramp, and the number attached in later telling. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: A is retrospective merchant memory **embedded in vendor prose** and self-conflicting — RR Auction's essay dates it **Spring 1976**, and a Tier-4 post attributed to the same merchant says **$525** wholesale, not $500; B is a documented null across ~13 MB with several independent advertisers on its side. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **first order UNKNOWN**; the *relationship* is evidenced, the *transaction* is not; the distribution account in §D.3/§F stands on the ramp. / RESIDUAL UNCERTAINTY: an invoice, Terrell's papers, or a Kilobaud/Creative Computing page could still settle it. / CONFIDENCE: High that the corpus holds no order; Low on the canon. / Maps: U-AP-2, U-C-4, U-A2-2, S1P1-CF-03, S1P2-CF-03.

**U.004 — THE PRICE, AND THE `666.66` STRING.** CLAIM A: the Apple-1 sold at **$666.66**. CLAIM B: the string is **verified absent from `sources/`**; the maker's only printed number is "a price under $700 at the retail level" (Wozniak, May 1977); and the corpus's only dollar-shaped 666 is a **competitor's** strike-through anchor — "IMSAI 8080 … a $666 value / only $595" (BYTE December 1976). / WHY THEY DIFFER: a distinctive price retails because it is distinctive; and the decoy sits in the same magazine, months earlier. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: A's carriers are a 2021 merchant conversation and a 2026 lot essay — **one memoir lineage in two wrappers**; B is a string census plus a founder band. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **price UNKNOWN as a figure; $666.66 stays FOUNDER CLAIM or UNKNOWN and never FACT**; the 6502-market comparator ($675 for a complete system) makes "under $700" market-rate, which bounds but does not establish it. / RESIDUAL: Kilobaud/Creative Computing text layers → U.037, U.038; the **decoy-as-origin hypothesis is an INFERENCE and is not adopted**. / CONFIDENCE: High on absence in these files; UNKNOWN on the true figure. / Maps: U-A2-4, S1P1-CF-03 (price leg), S1P2-03.

**U.005 — WAYNE'S PERCENTAGE, INTERVAL AND CONSIDERATION.** CLAIM A: **10%** on a 45/45/10 allocation, withdrawn after **eleven days** (the two catalogued dates, 1976-04-01 → 1976-04-12), consideration **$800** per the lot essay, or "$800 plus a later $1,500" (AP-20). CLAIM B: **12%** and "**12 days**", sold for $800, "no regrets" — the circulating canon. CLAIM C (record-integrity): **Ronald Wayne appears in no in-window document in any spelling**; his documented existence in this corpus is two signatures and later prose. / WHY THEY DIFFER: 45/45/10 exhausts 100, so 12% cannot coexist with the equal split; "12 days" rounds a dated instrument; the press printed no partnership news at all. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: A rests on a Tier-3 catalogue's reading of a Tier-1 object **this project has not read**; B rests on nothing citable; C is a counted census with a stated coverage gap. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **10%, eleven days — as the best-supported figures, not as verified clauses**; the consideration stays UNKNOWN; no average of 10 and 12 may be printed anywhere. / RESIDUAL: the clause text, the withdrawal papers, and whether Wayne's later litigation to sever his name from partnership debts is in any reachable docket → U.039, U.048. / CONFIDENCE: Medium-High on 10%; High that 12% is undocumented; Low on the money. / Maps: U-AP-4, U-C-2, S1P1-CF-07, S1P2-CF-01.

**U.006 — WHAT THE STARTING CAPITAL WAS, AND WHAT IT BOUGHT.** CLAIM A: a **$15,000** loan secured to fill the order (Wayne's retrospective account). CLAIM B: about **$1,000**, raised by selling an HP-65 for $500 and a VW bus for $750 (the *iWoz* lineage, AP-32). CLAIM C (record): no document of any family states either; contemporaneous print supplies only a **plausibility bound** — the HP-65's printed retail was **$795** (street $695), and the club's own used-system ask implies a **34.8%** discount, so `500/795 = 62.9%` against an observed `2000/3066.58 = 65.2%`. / WHY THEY DIFFER: two different purposes (board printing versus filling an order) conflated; both retrospective. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: neither side is Tier-1; only C is documentary, and it is arithmetic. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **capital UNKNOWN**; the print bounds one number's plausibility and corroborates no event. / RESIDUAL: any bank, lender or credit document; the $15,000 is not inconsistent with a *later* 1976–77 financing and may belong to §K's Markkula complex. / CONFIDENCE: Low (A), Low (B), Medium (C's bound). / Maps: U-AP-3, U-C-3, E-13/E-14.

**U.007 — MARKKULA.** CLAIM A (canon, post-1981 books): a **$91,000** bank guarantee, roughly **$250,000** in, a **26–28%** stake, arriving **February 1977**, as "the company's third co-founder". CLAIM B (corpus): **one 1981 sentence** — "A C Markkula, 32 years old, who took Apple from a garage operation to its current enviable position, also holds 8.3 million shares" — his only occurrence in ~13 MB, and **zero** occurrences in any file dated 1975 through December 1980; his arrival undated in every record held. / WHY THEY DIFFER: A descends from book history this project has not read; B is a print record silent on him **by design** — it printed share counts, not cap-table negotiations. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: none of A is documented here; B is a documented null plus one printed holding. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **a founder-sized block existed by 1980-12 (Medium-High as printed); terms UNKNOWN; entry date UNKNOWN; and the 1981 holding may not be back-projected into 1976–77 as capital.** / RESIDUAL: whether his arrival fell before or after 1977-01-03, which is the hinge of §N's last row and of any "capital forced incorporation" story. / CONFIDENCE: High for the null; Low for the canon. / Maps: S1P1-CF-06, S1P2-CF-02.

**U.008 — HOW THE MACHINE REACHED BUYERS.** CLAIM A: "sold **by word of mouth** throughout California and later nationwide through retail computer stores" (Wozniak, May 1977, first-party, contemporaneous). CLAIM B: the dated print ladder runs **New York (Sept 1976) → Berkeley mail order (Nov 1976) → exhibitor list (Dec 1976) → a ~35-store national directory including Tokyo (April 1977) → the maker's own bank-card direct-mail order form (June–July 1977)**; and no 1976 document evidences **any** authorised-dealer relationship, with the New York advertiser's own authorised-line block excluding Apple. / WHY THEY DIFFER: A compresses a sequence into a moral; B's directory describes **the retail circuit, not Apple's relation to it**; and B's best artifacts post-date the boundary. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: A is one founder's sentence; B is multiple unrelated dated artifacts plus two counted absences. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **the tension is the finding** — A's channel *type* is corroborated by print and its *sequence* is contradicted; the transition mechanism between the two states has no document, and §D.3's three candidate mechanisms remain equally consistent. / RESIDUAL: whether any dealer held Apple product under agreement before April 1977 is unestablished **either way**. / CONFIDENCE: High that both statements were printed; Low beyond that. / Maps: S1P1-CF-04, S1P2-CF-04, S1P1-17, S1P1-31, S1P1-34.

**U.009 — THE MONEY THAT IS NOT IN THE RECORD.** CLAIM A: first-year sales **$77,000** and FY1977 revenue **$770,000**, usually attributed to "the 1980 prospectus". CLAIM B: **no in-window or near-window text states any 1976 or 1977 revenue**; the printed series begins **FY1978** at $7.8M in one unsigned 1981 column; and the 1980 registration statement is paper-only behind the 1994-01-26 EDGAR floor. / WHY THEY DIFFER: the figures arrive as "prospectus figures" from parties who have never quoted the prospectus. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: neither side is evidenced; B is a documented null plus a single post-window witness. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **FY1976 and FY1977 revenue and profit UNKNOWN with reason**; $77,000 and $770,000 stay in this register and **never enter a metric table**. / RESIDUAL: only the paper prospectus or an IPO-era newspaper story can close it → U.041, U.038, U.050. / CONFIDENCE: High that no document carries them; UNKNOWN on the values. / Maps: S1P2-CF-05, U-A2-7, E-12.

**U.010 — ONE COLUMN, THREE PRECISIONS.** CLAIM A: FY1978 profit printed to the dollar (**$793,497**). CLAIM B: FY1979 and FY1980 printed to one or two significant figures (**$5M on $48M**, **$117M / $11.7M**), in the same column by the same hand. / WHY THEY DIFFER: mixed precision inside a single document indicates some rows taken from a printed source and some from memory or space — the column is investor-relations matter, not a filing. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: both are the same single witness; the conflict is internal. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **the exact FY1978 figure is not more reliable than the rounded later years merely because it looks so**; treat the whole series as printed statements of a recapitalisation. / RESIDUAL: which document, if any, the column's precise figure came from. / CONFIDENCE: High (both as printed). / Maps: U-A2-8.

**U.011 — THE OFFERING ARITHMETIC THAT DOES NOT FOOT.** CLAIM A: the December 1980 offering was **8%** of **52.4 million** shares. CLAIM B: it was **4,600,000** shares at **$22**, i.e. **8.78%**, and `4.6M × 22 = $101,200,000` against `0.08 × 52.4M × 22 = $92,240,000`; the same column then describes one holder's 8.3M shares as "well over $100 million" when its own arithmetic yields **$182,600,000**. / WHY THEY DIFFER: one figure is rounded, stale, or a different class or the over-allotment; the column mixes pre- and post-offering counts. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: single column, no filing available; internally checkable, not externally corroborable. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **carry both gross figures, prefer neither**, and do not use the column as a cap table beyond "three named holders at 8.3M each". / RESIDUAL: the paper prospectus → U.041. / CONFIDENCE: High (the arithmetic); Low (any reconciliation). **Merge action:** s1_p1 record **S1P1-10** points this subject at `S1P1-CF-09` (the issue-dating conflict); the pointer is a numbering slip and is re-keyed here to U.011 — the record's text is correct, its `Conflicts:` cell is not. / Maps: U-A2-9, U-C-5, S1P1-10.

**U.012 — WHAT BECAME OF THE PARTNERSHIP.** CLAIM A (assumed by the received story): the partnership **became** the corporation — one firm growing up. CLAIM B (record): **no document states** whether the 1976 agreement was terminated, assigned, absorbed or abandoned on 1977-01-03; whether partnership debts were assumed; or whether the three signatories received identical consideration. / WHY THEY DIFFER: continuity is the natural way to tell a survival; the archive holds two isolated instruments and one filing sentence. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: B is a counted absence across `sources/` plus two failed registry routes. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **two events, one sequence unrecorded**; the file's own boundary rule already refuses the single word "founded" (U.001). / RESIDUAL: the charter and any statement of information → U.042. / CONFIDENCE: High on the null; C-22 rates this the stage's most important unanswered legal question. / Maps: S1P1-CF-01 residual, C-01, C-22, S1P3-02.

**U.013 — THE NAME, FOUR TIMES.** CLAIM set: 1976 print gives "**Apple Computer Co**" (BYTE December 1976 editorial; Wozniak's May 1977 byline) and "**Apple Computers**" (Homebrew September 1976; the December exhibitor list); Apple's own June 1977 page says "**Apple Computer Inc.**"; a December 1980 third-party advertiser says "trademarks of the **Apple Corporation**". / WHY THEY DIFFER: publication lag, hearsay naming, copywriter looseness, and a real legal distinction the press did not observe. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: all four name forms are authentic as printed; only the company's own 1977 letterhead is authoritative for the corporation, and **no 1976–77 document names the partnership's registered style**. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **no name form may be cited without its carrier and date**; the word "partnership" does not occur in connection with Apple in any cached file. / RESIDUAL: the registered business name; whether "Apple Computer Company" was ever filed. / CONFIDENCE: High (as printed); Low (any inference about the entity from a name). / Maps: U-A2-1, U-A2-11, A2-74, C-04/C-05/C-06.

**U.014 — OFFICES OR DUTIES?** CLAIM A: **no officer title** for any principal occurs anywhere in the cached corpus — no president, vice president, chairman, treasurer, secretary, board of directors or officer is joined to an Apple name (C-13's exhaustive census). CLAIM B: the 1976 instrument allocated **functions** — Wayne "Mechanical Engineering and Documentation", Wozniak "Electrical Engineering", Jobs "Electrical Engineering **and Marketing**". / WHY THEY DIFFER: A is about offices, which trade press rarely printed; B is about duties, which a partnership deed records and no periodical would. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: A is a documented null with a stated coverage gap (BYTE Aug 1977–Nov 1980 uncached); B is one cataloguer's quotation of an unread document. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **no officer title is establishable in the Stage-1 record — which is not a finding that none existed**; a functional division of labour did exist, at the level the only 1976 paper records it. / RESIDUAL: the statement of information for the 1977 corporation (tried, unanswered) → U.042. / CONFIDENCE: High on the null as a null. / Maps: S1P1-CF-08, S1P1-22, S1P1-23.

**U.015 — WHOSE "FIRST".** CLAIM A: Terrell's BYTE Shop was "the **world's first computer store franchise**" (BYTE, July 1976). CLAIM B: Computer Mart of New York claimed the **first computer store on the East Coast** and, by May 1977, a new showroom listing APPLE among ~25 lines. CLAIM C: the mail-order route was already being used by club members in June 1976 without any store at all. / WHY THEY DIFFER: three interested parties, three different scopes (franchise / region / channel), none of them measurable against the others. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: all three are contemporaneous and all three are self-interested. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **primacy claims are treated as channel preconditions, never as ranked facts**; Apple's position in 1976 is *being stocked inside other people's primacy stories*. / RESIDUAL: whether any "first" is checkable against a directory. / CONFIDENCE: High that each was printed; no confidence in any ranking. / Maps: U-A2-5.

**U.016 — THE FAIRE'S OWN DATES.** CLAIM A: the first West Coast Computer Faire was billed for **April 15–17, 1977** (flyer and newsletter, December 1976 and February 1977). CLAIM B: a correspondent's July 1977 report says it was held "**April 16 and 17 of this year**". / WHY THEY DIFFER: a scheduled three-day billing versus a reader's days on the floor. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: A is promotional print with two printings (one campaign); B is a participant observation. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **"April 1977" is the safe form**; neither the 15th nor the 16th may be used to date anything in this volume, because the event lies outside the window in either case. / RESIDUAL: the promoter's official programme. / CONFIDENCE: Medium. / Maps: U-A2-3.

**U.017 — BITS OR BYTES.** CLAIM A: the cassette interface is specified at "**1500 bps**" (Apple's June 1977 advertisement). CLAIM B: transfer "**averages over 180 bytes per second**" (Wozniak, May 1977). / WHY THEY DIFFER: one measurement printed in two bases; `1500 / 8 = 187.5` bytes per second. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: both company-side, mutually consistent once the basis is stated. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **resolved on arithmetic, not on evidence** — the two are the same claim in different units; what remains open is only which basis each writer assumed for framing overhead. / RESIDUAL: real-world throughput with start/stop bits. / CONFIDENCE: High. / Maps: U-A2-12.

**U.018 — IS THE CORPUS'S SPINE CORRECTLY DATED?** CLAIM A: the cached 1976 BYTE issues are correctly month-dated and month confidence is High. CLAIM B: the July file **previews pieces appearing in "BYTE's August and September issues" while narrating events through "October 30"** — an internal impossibility. / WHY THEY DIFFER: A relies on Internet Archive metadata plus cross-references verified only for October–December; B is one of those cross-references failing. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: B is a specific observed contradiction; A is an assumption extended past its checks. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **October–December 1976 are safe; January–September 1976 carry `issue-date: provisional pending page-sequence verification`, month confidence Medium.** Nothing in the adopted boundary depends solely on the July file. / RESIDUAL: whether the item bundles later pages or the OCR layer mixes them — only a scan inspection settles it. / CONFIDENCE: High that the contradiction exists; UNKNOWN as to cause. / Maps: S1P1-CF-09, S1P1-30, D L-6.

**U.019 — WHAT COUNTS AS "INSIDE THE WINDOW".** CLAIM A: the April 1977 store directory and the June/July 1977 order form are **inside** the Stage-1 window (s1_p1 records S1P1-05, S1P1-06, minted before the boundary was argued). CLAIM B: the window closes **1977-01-03**, so both are **post-window witnesses** (§STAGE BOUNDARY JUSTIFICATION; S1P1-CF-10; S1P1-31). / WHY THEY DIFFER: an inherited implicit mid-1977 boundary versus the defended one. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: the artifacts' own cover dates are unambiguous; the disagreement is about the boundary, not the evidence. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **B**, because the defended boundary and the register's stage rows both carry 1977-01-03; the artifacts stay Tier-1 and are used, dated. / RESIDUAL: **a merge decision, not an evidentiary question** — if a later Stage-1 end is adopted, S1P1-05/06 become correct as written and the boundary section must be re-opened. / CONFIDENCE: High (labelling conflict resolvable by decision). **Merge action: correct S1P1-05 and S1P1-06's wording; do not delete the records.** / Maps: S1P1-CF-10.

**U.020 — A PROBE SUPPORT THAT DID NOT SURVIVE THE PRIMARY.** CLAIM A: Homebrew's newsletter of **1976-04-30** records Apple demonstrating to Homebrew, and anchors the start edge's confidence. CLAIM B: the paragraph sits under the standing column "**NOTES FROM THE NORTH**", whose subject is the **Sonoma County Micro Computer Club** meeting in Cotati; Homebrew **reprinted another club's letter** ("In April the APPLE 6502 system was our special guest. We are grateful to STEVE WOZNIAK for providing transportation."). / WHY THEY DIFFER: a reprint is mistaken for a first-party report when the column heading is not read. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: B is the primary text; A is a derivation from it. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: the document remains the **earliest dated naming of the machine with a founder**, which is a strong fact; "Apple demoed at Homebrew in April 1976" is **unsupported and not used**. / RESIDUAL: whether the machine was ever shown to Homebrew at all — not establishable on this corpus. / CONFIDENCE: High. / Maps: D L-3, probe A fact-list #4, S1P1-14.

**U.021 — SILENCE, OR INACTIVITY?** CLAIM A: the founding period's best outside primary source stopped mentioning Apple at the end of 1976, which suggests something happened. CLAIM B: Homebrew `hcc0211` (1976-12-10) and `hcc0213` (1977-01-19) carry **zero** body occurrences after five consecutive 1976 issues carried the name, and BYTE's 1977 Apple coverage resumes only in April — two independent silences with no shared cause. / WHY THEY DIFFER: a 4–8 page volunteer bulletin and a monthly magazine with an advertising calendar; neither is a corporate record. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: both absences are counted; neither can carry an inference about activity. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **report the silences, price them, and draw nothing from them**; the incorporation month is empty because paper is thin, not because January 1977 was quiet. / RESIDUAL: the eight uncached Homebrew Vol 2 numbers and BYTE Aug 1977–Nov 1980 → U.044, U.045. / CONFIDENCE: High (the counts); **no confidence** in any behavioural reading. / Maps: C-20, S1P1-29, A2-76.

**U.022 — NUMBERS THAT ARE ONLY PROMOTERS' NUMBERS.** CLAIM A: the Faire announced **12,800** attendees against ~**200** exhibitors and a billed 7,000–10,000 expectation. CLAIM B: every one of these is a promoter's self-report; the corpus holds **no independent count** of any of them, and the club's own attendance figures are an organiser's survey. / WHY THEY DIFFER: counted demand and announced demand are different objects, and only one was ever printed. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: single-source self-reports on both sides. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **use as market-as-knowable (§H) and never as demand**; a ratio such as `12800/8500 = 1.51×` is an arithmetic relation between two forecasts, not growth. / RESIDUAL: any third-party count. / CONFIDENCE: High (as printed); Low (as measurement). / Maps: A2-43, A2-11, A2-61.

**U.023 — HOW MANY WITNESSES STAND BEHIND THE ONLY PRINTED FIGURES?** CLAIM A: the FY1978–FY1980 series is corroborated by the BYTE column **and** a Library-of-Congress page. CLAIM B: the LoC page is a **2023 page citing 2001–2020 retellings** — a derivative copy, not a second origin. / WHY THEY DIFFER: two independent-looking carriers can share one downstream source, and §3's independence rule decides by *origin*, not by *host*. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: B. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **the entire printed fiscal series for Apple's first years rests on ONE unsigned 1981 column**; confidence is capped at what a single document supports, and every downstream use must say so. / RESIDUAL: the paper prospectus is the only independent carrier that likely ever existed → U.041. / CONFIDENCE: High (the demotion). / Maps: AP-23 demotion, S1P1-21.

**U.024 — "ABOUT 200 APPLE-1s".** CLAIM A: roughly **200** units were produced; some accounts split 150–200, or "50 for the Byte Shop first". CLAIM B: the figure traces to a wiki-derived count whose only cited support is a **2011 Wikipedia retrieval**, and the collector registry publishes **surviving serials** — artifact scholarship that cannot bound production without a capture-recapture model this project does not have. / WHY THEY DIFFER: survivor counts and production counts are different objects; serial gaps look like totals to a general reader. / EVIDENCE WEIGHT: A has no document; B is a provenance trace plus a methodological objection. / BEST-SUPPORTED INTERPRETATION: **production and sales volumes UNKNOWN**; the registry is usable for configuration and survival, not for totals. / RESIDUAL: a production ledger, which almost certainly never existed. / CONFIDENCE: High (undocumented); Low (the 200). / Maps: D L-9, `sources/apple1registry_stories.txt`.

### U.iii Documented nulls (each carries the search that failed, by name)

U.025 **Record-integrity defects, minted as nulls so they cannot be inherited silently.** The probe's provenance headers cause false positives in **ten of thirteen** Homebrew files when lines 1–5 are not excluded (A2-05, A2-77), including all five issues on which AP-26's 1975 silence rests; and s1_p1 record S1P1-10 mis-points its `Conflicts:` cell at S1P1-CF-09 (see U.011). Both are FACTS about this archive, verified by re-running the stated search. Confidence: High. Follow-up: any re-run must adopt the header-exclusion recipe and re-check every cached file's provenance block.

U.026 **No in-window printed price or list for the first product.** Exhaustive token pass over twelve 1976 BYTE issues plus Homebrew 1975-11 → 1977-01 (headers excluded); the only maker-side number is a May 1977 band. A manufacturer's list **existed** (a dealer discounts against it in November 1976) and is printed nowhere. Not establishable in the Stage-1 record. Routes: U.037, U.038, U.051. Confidence: High on the null.

U.027 **No order, invoice, shipment, payment, reorder or return document of any kind.** A2-62's extent statement; the corpus names dealers who carried the product and never names a transaction. Not a finding that none occurred. Routes: U.039, U.040, U.048. Confidence: High.

U.028 **No in-window trace of a capital event or of Markkula.** Zero occurrences of the name before February 1981 across 31 cached files; the terms appear in no retrieved document. Confidence: High on the null, UNKNOWN on the facts. Routes: U.038, U.045, U.050.

U.029 **No in-window trace of Ronald Wayne.** Zero body occurrences in 16 BYTE issues and 13 newsletters (23 unrelated hits itemised at C-21). A statement about the source class, not about the man's activity. Confidence: High within these files, Medium overall. Route: U.048.

U.030 **Maker-print absence: what non-printing does and does not establish.** Apple placed **no** advertisement of its own in any 1976 document (AP-22, re-confirmed A2-18/A2-65); the first product appears in no maker advertisement after May 1977 with **no** discontinuation notice or remaining-stock statement. Non-use is documented; decision, capacity and intent are not, and the three are indistinguishable on this record. Confidence: Medium-High on the absences (logotype OCR risk), UNKNOWN on every reading of them.

U.031 **No officer or governance record.** C-13's census; registry route unanswered (C-07). The earliest documented officer of Apple Computer, Inc. is not establishable from any retrieved document. Route: U.042. Confidence: High as a null, Medium as a general statement.

U.032 **No 1975 document naming the machine, the venture or either founder.** Searched: `hcc0109`, `hcc0110`, and the six cached 1976-01→06 BYTE files, headers excluded. **1975-03 → 1975-10 of the club run is not on disk**, so the null is bounded, not general. Routes: U.044, U.051. Confidence: High within the cached span.

U.033 **The 1976-11-20 demonstration is a single friendly witness, published five months later.** No second origin; Helmers was a friend of the subjects and BYTE was commercially engaged in the category (four issues of Byte Shop vendor advertising precede Apple's first appearance). The event is used; observer neutrality is not assumed. Confidence: Medium-High.

U.034 **No in-window intellectual-property record.** Earliest Apple-assigned US application is 1977-04-11 (post-window, corporation-named); the first product was never patented. The register read was **non-exhaustive** — page 2 of the result set was not examined — so this is a **search null, not a register null**. Route: U.053. Confidence: Medium-High.

U.035 **The partnership's liability posture is unrecorded.** No 1976 document asserts limited liability; the "no corporate protection" statement is a 2022 participant memory. The record does not show exposure being *accepted* as a trade-off; it shows nobody writing about it. Confidence: High on the absence, Low-Medium on the posture.

U.036 **The negative-reception layer is absent as a class.** No complaint, returns, warranty, cancellation or critical review of either machine in-window; the source group printed almost none. §M's thinness measures the press, not the venture. Routes: U.045, U.050. Confidence: High.

### U.iv Untried routes (named, never silently dropped; the verbatim hand-offs are in the next section)

U.037 **Kilobaud, September and November 1976 — text layer.** **UNTRIED, and a FETCH REQUEST the earlier parts handed back and did not fetch.** The title most likely to carry an Apple-placed 1976 price line; the cached items have no text layer. Route: `archive.org/metadata/Kilobaud197609` → intake to `sources/` → local OCR → grep with headers excluded. Would settle U.004, U.026. **Do not run from this part: web budget 0.**

U.038 **Creative Computing, 1976 (and its 1977–1981 IPO text).** **UNTRIED / not fetched.** A named untried family in the probe's own data-gaps table (DG-4, DG-10, AP-34); the plausible printed carrier of FY1976–77 columns and of a Markkula people-item. Query: `"Creative Computing" 1976 "Apple" price advertisement`; `Creative Computing 1977 1978 Apple revenue prospectus`. Would settle U.004, U.009, U.028.

U.039 **Christie's lot 242 full instrument text; Sotheby's New York 2011-12-13 lot 241.** **UNANSWERED then UNTRIED.** The 2026 lot fetch returned **403** (artnet); the 2011 catalogue of the **same object**, fifteen years earlier, is the likeliest place to find transcribed clauses and images of both 1976 papers, and was never requested. Would settle U.005, U.006, U.012, U.014.

U.040 **RR Auction's 50th-anniversary lot clause.** **UNTRIED / not fetched.** The route C's own research-debt line assigns for a transcribed withdrawal clause; vendor prose, so it must be treated as U.003's lineage, not as a new witness.

U.041 **The paper-only 1980 registration statement and prospectus, FY1976–1977 columns.** **UNTRIED / not fetched**, and unreachable by the scripted EDGAR route (floor **1994-01-26** for this registrant). Route: SEC Public Reference Room or National Archives II paper accession search, File No. 2-74442-era; or a newspaper carrying the prospectus extract. Would settle U.009, U.011, U.023, U.026.

U.042 **California Secretary of State entity file; the 1977 charter and any statement of information.** **UNANSWERED** (API non-response, C-07), with the paper route **UNTRIED**; the company's own 1994 exhibit index names the SoS as the charter registry, so the document very likely exists. A "Utah filing" hypothesis was tried and produced nothing (C-26) and must not be re-run as though untried. Would settle U.012, U.014, U.031.

U.043 **Santa Clara County recorder/assessor: 20211 Crist Drive and the Cupertino suites.** **UNANSWERED** (portal **403/000**, AP-36); the paper or microfilm route is UNTRIED. Would date the garage and the first premises, hence U.002.

U.044 **Homebrew Computer Club Newsletter: Vol 1 No 1–8 (1975-03 → 1975-10) and Vol 2 No 8, 10, 12, 14–21.** **UNTRIED** — only 13 of 32 enumerated items are cached. The 1975 run is exactly where a late-1975 demonstration would have been printed, so it bears on U.032 and on the start edge. Query is recorded verbatim in the next section.

U.045 **BYTE, August 1977 → November 1980.** **UNTRIED** — not on disk; the stage's whole later half is unmined, and the corpus's 1981 endpoint is reached across a three-year gap. Bears on U.009, U.021, U.028, U.029, U.036.

U.046 ***Business Week*, 1976-07-12, the Byte Shop feature named in 1976 print.** **UNTRIED** — dated, named, and unretrieved (A2-10; DG-11); the only general-business-press item the corpus points to by name.

U.047 **Component procurement, contract assembly and UCC filings.** **UNTRIED** as a family: chip-distributor and broker records, California trade-journal profiles of hobby-board assembly houses, and any online UCC-1 for Santa Clara County 1976–77. Would settle U.041's supply twin in §G.

U.048 **Court records: Wayne's later suit to clear his name; RECAP pre-1990; the name dispute's earliest filings.** **UNTRIED beyond one index search** that returned nothing before 2003-10-08 (C-06). Bears on U.005 and U.029; firewall: the 2003 posture is not evidence about 1976.

U.049 **Terrell's Polaroids read at image level; an Apple-I manual, board silkscreen or box sheet.** **UNTRIED.** Would date a first showing and give a first-party artifact for configuration and price (AP-30; `sources/apple1registry_stories.txt` publishes serials, not manuals).

U.050 **Apple's own press releases 1976–1980, and general-interest press coverage.** **EMPTY / UNTRIED**: the company's own web record begins 1996-10-22 (AP-08); Chronicling America returned **403** in the probe and the newspaper back-files are unattempted. Bears on U.009, U.028, U.036.

U.051 **A local OCR run over the cached Kilobaud JP2s.** **UNTRIED and inside the project's own reach** — no web request needed, only the tesseract route, and no OCR toolchain was in this part's write scope. The cheapest single route to U.004.

U.052 **The unbuilt periodical families: *InfoWorld*, *73 Magazine*, *Byte* pre-1976, *People's Computer Company*, *Softalk*, *Radio Electronics*.** **UNTRIED in this stage entirely.** Method §14 rule 6 makes an untried family **not** a null, and §H/§I/§L are bounded by this row.

U.053 **A full-text/assignment search of the patent register.** **UNTRIED at second page**: the first read was non-exhaustive, so U.034 is a search null and not a register null.

U.054 **`tools/` harvester run records and Internet Archive metadata (page-sequence verification).** **UNTRIED and not to be moved** — the July 1976 impossibility at U.018 is closed only by inspecting the item's page sequence, which these records and the IA metadata can do; `_MANIFEST.md` places them off-limits for tidy operations.

**U.v Coverage note, and the one thing this section claims.** §U mints **fifty-five** anchors: **24** live conflicts plus **U.055** (appended, not inserted), **12** documented nulls, and **18** named untried routes. It claims no resolution that the record does not support, and it reopens nothing the released parts settled. **Record-selection null (§2), §U:** the conflicts that survive are the ones two record-keeping traditions happen to have printed — trade advertising and an auction catalogue. There is no register of the arguments nobody wrote down, and a conflict table built only from surviving paper will always look tidier than the venture was.
